--- Commit Message ---
LU-19276 llapi: add llapi_changelog_(de)register

Add llapi_changelog_register() and llapi_changelog_deregister()
to allow them to be used by external applications.

Move CHANGELOG_USER_NAMELEN and CHANGELOG_USER_NAMELEN_FULL to
lustre_user.h since applications need to know the buffer limits
when registering a changelog user.

Man pages llapi_changelog_register/deregister.3 are added and
lctl-changelog_register/deregister.8 are updated to reflect the
new changes. sanity.sh/test_160q is modified to verify this patch.

Move llapi_obdname2devno() and llapi_ioctl_dev() into internal
library functions so that they are more easily usable, but not
yet fully public API functions.

Since llapi_ioctl_dev() requires the OBD device to be registered
in order to call l_ioctl(), have llapi_obdname2devno() call
llapi_register_ioc_dev() internally to ensure it is done instead
of depending on the caller.  Change llapi_register_ioc_dev() so
that it can be called repeatedly without closing and opening the
OBD ioctl device each time.

Rename yaml_get_device_index() to llapi_yaml_get_device_index()
to avoid namespace clash with yaml library. Fix a bug with that
code which returned 'device 1' on error, and leaked resources
on error cleanup paths.

Remove long unused jt_lcfg_device() wrapper function.

Patch Set 2:

I attempted to fix the build errors present in this patch and succeeded. Yay! Now, keep in mind I barely have any idea what I am doing, so please doublecheck the changes I made to make sure they make sense.

This service is only provided in hopes to be useful and reduce latency from push to getting actual results in the face of trivial code errors

Here's summary of my changes:
The change in this patch addresses a compile warning or error related to a type mismatch in the `liblustreapi_chlg.c` file. Specifically:

- **Before**: `data.ioc_inlbuf1 = username;`  
  This assigns a `const char *` (pointer to constant string) directly to `ioc_inlbuf1`, which is likely declared as a `char *` (non-const pointer), causing a warning or error in strict compilation modes.

- **After**: `data.ioc_inlbuf1 = (char *)username;`  
  The cast `(char *)username` explicitly converts the `const char *` to `char *`, resolving the compilation error by suppressing the warning about discarding `const` qualifier.

### Summary:
The patch adds an explicit cast to silence a compilation error caused by assigning a `const char *` to a `char *` field. This is a common fix when interfacing with legacy or kernel API structures that don't use `const` correctly, ensuring the code compiles cleanly without violating type safety in a way that would cause runtime issues.

Patch Set 12:

I attempted to fix the build errors present in this patch and succeeded. Yay! Now, keep in mind I barely have any idea what I am doing, so please doublecheck the changes I made to make sure they make sense.

This service is only provided in hopes to be useful and reduce latency from push to getting actual results in the face of trivial code errors

Here's summary of my changes:
## Explanation of the Fix

**The Problem:**
The compile error occurred in `lustre/utils/mkfs_lustre.c` at line 530 due to incorrect argument ordering in the `fprintf()` call.

**The Error:**
```c
fprintf(stderr,
    "%s: filesystem name '%s' must be 1-%d chars: %s\n",
    progname, LUSTRE_MAXFSNAME, optarg, strerror(-rc));
```

The format string expected:
- `%s` → progname ✓
- `%s` → filesystem name (should be `optarg`)
- `%d` → maximum length (should be `LUSTRE_MAXFSNAME`)
- `%s` → error message ✓

But the arguments were:
- progname ✓
- **LUSTRE_MAXFSNAME** (int) - passed to 2nd `%s` (expects char*)
- **optarg** (char*) - passed to `%d` (expects int)
- strerror(-rc) ✓

**The Fix:**
I swapped the order of `LUSTRE_MAXFSNAME` and `optarg` to match the format string:

```c
fprintf(stderr,
    "%s: filesystem name '%s' must be 1-%d chars: %s\n",
    progname, optarg, LUSTRE_MAXFSNAME, strerror(-rc));
```

**Rationale:**
This matches the format specifiers with their corresponding arguments, ensuring type safety and correct output. The error message will now properly display the filesystem name followed by the maximum allowed length.

--- Message (Gerrit AI review for Lustre, 2026-07-28 00:57:12.000000000) ---
Patch Set 17:

(19 comments)

AI code review results below, please doublecheck

Some concerns, see inline comments. The new llapi entry points come bundled with an independent rework of OBD device-name resolution (llapi_obdname2devno(), yaml_get_device_index() moved into liblustreapi, parse_devname()/lcfg_set_devname() signature changes, do_name2dev()/jt_lcfg_device() removed) that changes behaviour for every 'lctl --device NAME' caller, not just changelogs. That part is independently landable and would be easier to review as its own patch. There is also no test that calls llapi_changelog_register()/llapi_changelog_deregister() directly - test_160q only exercises the lctl command path, so the new library API itself is untested.

Some concerns, see inline comments.
